## DELIVERY-SHAPE CONTRACT SCHEMA REPORT

### 1. Directory Layout Convention

**Structure:**
```
<initiative-slug>/
├── README.md                  (root: goal + KRs + tree + hand-count manifest oracle)
├── D<n>-<slug>/               (deliverable directory)
│   ├── _deliverable.md        (layer header: front-matter + statement)
│   └── N<nn>-<slug>.md        (node file, one node per file)
└── …
```

**Rules:**
- Numeric prefixes (`D1`, `N03`) provide deterministic reading/walking order; node numbers padded (`N01`, not `N1`)
- Layer-header files underscore-prefixed (`_deliverable.md`) to sort first and disambiguate from node files
- Node files are the only `N*.md` files; one node per file
- Root `README.md` is the single top-down entry point carrying goal + KRs + rendered tree + **hand-count manifest** (oracle the walk-script reproduces)

**Tracker mapping:**
- Deliverable (`D*/_deliverable.md`) → milestone-class
- Node (`N*.md`) → issue-class  
- Task (`- [ ]`/`- [x]` checklist line inside node) → sub-issue-class

---

### 2. Cross-Reference Convention

**Three reinforcing signals, no hand-maintained index:**

- **Down** — parent links to each child by relative path (`_deliverable.md` lists nodes; `README.md` renders whole tree)
- **Up** — every file's front-matter carries `parent:` (the enclosing layer) so a reader starting at any node can climb without scanning directories
- **To the bet** — every deliverable and node carries `serves_kr:` naming the KR it serves, so the outcome trace is explicit at every layer

**Manifest derivation:**
- The manifest is **derived, never authored** — a walk-script reconstructs milestone/issue/sub-issue counts from directory structure + front-matter
- The hand-count in the README exists only as the oracle the derived manifest is checked against

---

### 3. Per-Node Tags Schema (Front-Matter)

**Load-bearing tags (all layers carry):**

| Tag | Values | Required/Optional | Notes |
|-----|--------|-------------------|-------|
| `layer` | `deliverable` \| `node` | **Required** | which layer this file is |
| `id` | `D1` \| `N03` | **Required** | stable identifier within the plan |
| `title` | `<one line>` | **Required** | |
| `parent` | `<relative path or id of enclosing layer>` | **Required** | enables climbing without scanning dirs |
| `serves_kr` | `KR<n>` | **Required** (deliverable); required on nodes | deliverable↔KR and node↔KR trace; every deliverable serves exactly one KR |
| `maps_to` | `<tracker-artefact class>` | **Required** | milestone / issue / sub-issue class |

**Node-specific tags:**

| Tag | Values | Required/Optional | Notes |
|-----|--------|-------------------|-------|
| `type` | `spike` \| `story` \| `adr` \| `experiment` \| `ktlo` \| `capability` (and to-fill: `slo`, `migration`, `deprecation`, `incident`, `compliance`) | **Required (node files only)** | polymorphic node discriminator; selects completion-criterion form and delegation target |
| `skeleton` | `true` \| (absent) | **Optional** | NODE-LEVEL: is this the walking-skeleton node? (task-level marked with backtick tag `` `skeleton` `` inside checklist line) |
| `acceptance` | `true` \| (absent) | **Optional** | NODE-LEVEL: does this node verify its parent's cross-seam criterion? (task-level marked with backtick tag `` `acceptance` `` inside checklist line); maps to Linear label; close acceptance node last to align milestone 100% with aggregate verification |
| `external_window` | `<external constraint>` \| `none` | **Optional** | never effort-in-days; only genuine external constraints (deprecation notice, legal deadline, canary soak, embargo) |
| `delegates_to` | `<rule or skill that owns this node type's discipline>` | **Optional** | named delegation target |

**Completion criterion structure (nested):**

```yaml
completion:
  form: <completion-criterion form>  # type-appropriate (see menu below)
  criterion: <the observable criterion, by form>
  verifies_parent: <parent-layer-id> | (absent)  # when acceptance: true — which parent's criterion this node closes
```

**Completion-criterion forms (the menu):**
- `acceptance-criteria` (story)
- `decision+stop-condition` (spike)
- `decision-record` (adr)
- `hypothesis+success-metric` (experiment)
- `numeric-target` (slo)
- `rollback-per-phase` (migration)
- `the-capability-spec` (capability)
- `none` (ktlo carve-out)

---

### 4. Manifest Schema

**Manifest is derived, not hand-authored.** Walk-script reconstructs from directory structure + front-matter.

**Three artefact classes** (tracker classes the conversion produces):

| Layer | File source | Manifest class | Maps to (Linear) | Fields derived from |
|-------|-------------|-----------------|-----------------|---------------------|
| Deliverable | `D*/_deliverable.md` | milestone-class | `linear-milestone` | `title` (name); initiative (project); directory nesting |
| Node | `N*.md` | issue-class | `linear-issue` | `title` (issue title); `type` and `serves_kr` → labels; directory nesting → milestone parent |
| Task | `- [ ]`/`- [x]` line | sub-issue-class | sub-issue | task text (title); file nesting → parent issue; `[x]` → done-state |

**Per-node tags propagate to manifest:**
- `type` (polymorphic discriminator) rides on issue as a label
- `serves_kr` (outcome spine) rides on issue as a label
- `completion.form` and `completion.criterion` ride as issue description / metadata (orthogonal, not load-bearing on structural shape)
- `skeleton` and `acceptance` flags map to Linear labels (orthogonal to structure)

**No hand-invention at conversion:** every field Linear needs is sourced from front-matter or directory nesting.

---

## GATE DECISION (ADR 0001)

**Title:** "`delivery-shape`: a new skill, not an expansion of `planning-and-task-breakdown`"

**Status:** Accepted  
**Date:** 2026-05-25

**Decision:** **(A) Build a standalone `delivery-shape` skill.**

**Boundary (what DS owns vs delegates):**

**`delivery-shape` owns:**
- Committed-initiative → deliverable/node hierarchy
- The outcome spine (`serves_kr` at every layer)
- Node-type selection and completion-form-by-type
- Rule A1 design-doc branch
- The walkable file-set contract
- The tracker-manifest seam

**`delivery-shape` does NOT own:**
- Per-node task breakdown → **delegates down to `planning-and-task-breakdown`** (already recorded in each story node's `delegates_to`)
- Per-node-type discipline → **delegates to the owning rule/skill** (select and delegate; never re-author)

**Justification:**

The node layer is **polymorphic with eight completion-criterion forms** (`acceptance-criteria`, `decision+stop-condition`, `decision-record`, `hypothesis+success-metric`, `numeric-target`, `rollback-per-phase`, `the-capability-spec`, `none`), each selecting a delegation target. The spike's plan exercises five of them on real content (`spike`, `story`, `adr`, `experiment`, `ktlo`). **`planning-and-task-breakdown` has no concept of node type or completion-form selection** — it knows only "task with an acceptance criterion." This polymorphic vocabulary is both too large and too far from "task breakdown" to bolt onto PTB without making PTB two skills wearing one frontmatter.

**Consequences (relevant to D2/D3):**

- PTB keeps its clean single-input/single-output contract; DS layers above it with low coupling
- The polymorphic node vocabulary lives in one place with a grounding rule against fabricating unexercised types
- DS→PTB delegation is a clean seam: DS picks node types; PTB does per-node task breakdown
- **Two skills now occupy adjacent territory; their trigger descriptions must be disambiguated** (KR3 gates on 0 collisions with `planning-and-task-breakdown`)
- DS must be authored to the full skill-anatomy spec and registered in README + `using-this-pack` flowchart
- The to-fill node-type appendix (`slo`, `migration`, `deprecation`, `incident`, `compliance`, `capability`) is unexercised until an initiative grounds each

---

## SKILL-ANATOMY SPEC (Required Sections and Frontmatter)

**Frontmatter fields (in `---` YAML block):**

```yaml
name: <skill-name>
description: <1-2 sentences, includes trigger phrases>
pack: <product | engineering | meta>
lifecycle_stage: <define | plan | build | ship | verify | review | meta | reference>
principles_implemented:
  - source: <eng-universal | eng-agentic | roadmap | product>
    id: <principle or rule id>
    bucket: <embedded | standalone | hook | sub-agent>
length_target: <e.g. 200-280>
author: Anton Babushkin
predecessor:
  repo: <URL or "none">
  skill: <skill name in source repo, or "none">
  relation: <derivative | adjacent | new>
kept_from_predecessor: <list, or "n/a">
changed_from_predecessor: <list, or "n/a">
```

**Required sections (in this exact order):**

1. **Title** — `# <Skill Name>`
2. **Purpose** — one paragraph
3. **When to use** — explicit trigger phrases and intents, with size-the-work rubric where relevant
4. **When not to use** — explicit non-triggers with the right alternative skill named
5. **Inputs** — what the skill expects to be given
6. **Outputs** — what it produces, including the artefact path under `docs/`
7. **Workflow** — numbered steps with hard gates marked `[GATE]`
8. **Artefact template** — the markdown the skill produces (may be retitled per artefact type)
9. **Common rationalisations** — table of typical excuses and rebuttals
10. **Red flags** — bullet list of signs the skill is being violated
11. **Verification / exit criteria** — how to know the skill ran correctly
12. **References** — pointers to `references/` and to source principles

**Length target:** 100–300 lines (hard cap 350; beyond 350 requires split).

---

**Key observations for D2 authoring:**

- D2 builds the `delivery-shape` skill, which must declare `pack: engineering` (or check the pattern in the pack value list)
- The skill's lifecycle stage will be somewhere in `define | plan | build | ship | verify | review` depending on current status
- The `principles_implemented` must cite relevant rules from the universal/agentic principles
- `predecessor` will likely be `{ repo: "none", skill: "none", relation: "new" }` since this is a novel skill
- The frontmatter and all 12 sections are **required** (no exemptions for engineering-pack skills; only meta-pack skills are exempt from the outputs/template/red-flags/verification sections)
```

## DELIVERY PLAN INVENTORY REPORT

### 1. PLAN STRUCTURE & CONVENTIONS (README.md)

**Plan Structure:**
The delivery plan uses a three-layer hierarchy mapping to tracker artefacts:
- **Initiative** (README) → goal + KRs
- **Deliverable** (D*/_deliverable.md) → milestone (tagged with `serves_kr`)
- **Node** (N*.md) → issue (polymorphic: spike/story/adr/experiment/ktlo/capability)
- **Task** (- [ ] checkbox lines) → sub-issue

**Conventions:**
- Directory nesting encodes hierarchy; numeric `D*/N*` prefixes ensure deterministic ordering
- Cross-references use relative paths + front-matter `parent` / `serves_kr` back-links
- Deliverables map to Linear milestones and nodes map to Linear issues via `maps_to: linear-{milestone,issue}` tags
- Node types are polymorphic: each carries the completion criterion its work requires (spike: decision+stop-condition; story: acceptance-criteria; adr: decision-record; experiment: hypothesis+success-metric; ktlo: none/carve-out)
- Two orthogonal flags applied on top of type:
  - **`skeleton: true`** (front-matter + task tag) — opens end-to-end path; foundational work folded in
  - **`acceptance: true`** (front-matter + task tag) — closes parent's cross-seam criterion; only exists when parent's Done is irreducible to ∀child.done

---

### 3. D2 NODE INVENTORY (N04–N07)

#### N04: Decompose initiative → deliverables → nodes → tasks, AC by default

**Type:** story | **Parent:** D2 | **Serves KR:** KR2 | **Maps to:** linear-issue

**What it delivers:**
Decomposes goal + KRs into deliverables → nodes → tasks, with every story node carrying acceptance criteria emitted by default so users stop getting low-level tasks that are missing the "how do I know this is done?" check.

**Acceptance Criteria (verbatim):**
- Done when: given a fed initiative (goal + KRs as text), the skill emits a file-set whose every `story` node carries an Acceptance-criteria block — checked by `bin/check-plan-framing`.
- Done when: every deliverable carries a `serves_kr` tag and every node carries a `type` tag, so the hierarchy is walkable (N02's script succeeds on the emitted plan).

**Dependencies:** N02 (walk-script validates the emitted plan)

**Per-Node Metadata/Tags:**
- Front-matter: `skeleton: false` (implied; no skeleton flag present)
- Tasks tagged: `[ ] skeleton —` and `[ ]` (standard task)
- Delegates to: `planning-and-task-breakdown (per-node task breakdown)`

---

#### N05: Foundational-work prompt folded into the walking skeleton

**Type:** story | **Parent:** D2 | **Serves KR:** KR2 | **Maps to:** linear-issue

**What it delivers:**
The skill asks "what toolchain/setup must exist for the skeleton to run?" and folds the answer into the walking-skeleton task, ensuring foundational work is never silently dropped and never appears as a setup task before the skeleton.

**Acceptance Criteria (verbatim):**
- Done when: for an initiative needing toolchain setup, the emitted plan contains exactly one `skeleton`-flagged task whose description includes the foundational/toolchain work — and **no** separate setup task precedes it. Verified by `bin/check-plan-framing` (≥1 skeleton task; 0 pre-skeleton setup tasks).

**Dependencies:** N02 (walk-script validates); N04 (decomposition must be in place)

**Per-Node Metadata/Tags:**
- Front-matter: no model/review/skill tags present
- Tasks tagged: `[ ] Add the foundational-work prompt…` and `[ ] Guard against a pre-skeleton setup task…`
- Delegates to: `planning-and-task-breakdown (walking-skeleton-first rule; no pre-skeleton setup task)`

---

#### N06: Rule A1 design-doc branch + up/down delegation

**Type:** story | **Parent:** D2 | **Serves KR:** KR2 | **Maps to:** linear-issue

**What it delivers:**
The skill branches on the Rule A1 design-doc trigger and delegates up to `initiative-shape` and down to `planning-and-task-breakdown`, so it never re-authors sub-skill gates (guarantees drift, agentic P7) and a design-doc-worthy deliverable gets a design doc before tasks.

**Acceptance Criteria (verbatim):**
- Done when: a deliverable that meets a Rule A1 trigger routes to `design-doc` before task breakdown; one that does not proceeds on goal + deliverables. Both branches exercised in a test plan.
- Done when: the skill cites `initiative-shape` (up) and `planning-and-task-breakdown` (down) by current name and contains no copied gate text from either — checked by grep against the two SKILL.md files.

**Dependencies:** N04 (decomposition must handle both branches); N02 (walk-script validates)

**Per-Node Metadata/Tags:**
- Front-matter: no model/review/skill tags present
- Delegates to: `design-doc (Rule A1 trigger); initiative-shape (upstream goal+KRs); planning-and-task-breakdown (downstream tasks)`

---

**Type:** experiment | **Parent:** D2 | **Serves KR:** KR2 | **Maps to:** linear-issue | **`acceptance: true`** | **Verifies parent:** D2

- D2: N04, N05, N06, N07 — marked with `[ ]` (open)

**Conclusion:** Model, review, and skill tags would need to be **assigned manually** to the node files' front-matter if needed; they are not currently part of the delivery-shape contract and are not encoded in the manifest output.

> AGENT

Good — binary review it is, and you want me to reason out the task-representation setup rather than just pick. Let me do that thinking, since it'll become a rule.

**The task-representation question: node→issue, tasks→checklist (recommended).**

The deciding principle comes from your own `rules/linear-workflow.md`: the **issue is the lifecycle unit** — review → fix → commit+push → summary comment → Done runs *per issue*. A node has a completion criterion (acceptance-criteria, decision+stop-condition, hypothesis+success-metric, …); that criterion *is* the issue's Done. The leaf tasks are the steps toward it — they share one Done-cycle, so they belong as a `- [ ]` checklist in the issue body, not as 11 separate objects each demanding its own review/commit/push/comment cycle.

Sub-issues earn their place only when a leaf needs its **own** Done-cycle (own acceptance criterion, own commit). But in this 3-level contract, that's precisely the signal that the leaf isn't a task — it's another *node*, and its parent is a *capability*. So the clean invariant is: **if a task wants to be a sub-issue, re-model it as a node; otherwise it's a checklist line.** This batch's tasks are 1–2 tightly-coupled steps per node, none independently shippable → checklist, 6 issues. This also matches how D1 was actually run (ABA-282 has zero sub-issues).

Let me write up the full plan.

N07 — skill must exist)\n8. **N09** — blocked by N08\n\n(N05 and N06 are siblings on N04 — parallelizable, but listed/created sequentially.)\n\n## Per-issue body template\n\nEach issue description carries, in order:\n- **One-liner** — what the node delivers.\n- **Serves:** D{n} → KR{n}.\n- **Acceptance criteria** — verbatim from the node file (the issue's Done).\n- **Tasks** — the leaf tasks as a `- [ ]` checklist (mark the `skeleton`/`acceptance` task where flagged).\n- **Blocked by** — prior issue identifier(s), or \"ready\".\n- **Source** — `examples/delivery-plans/top-down-delivery-planning/D{n}-…/N{nn}-….md`.\n- **Delegates to** — the skill the node hands work to (from `delegates_to`).\n\nAC / task text is taken verbatim from the node files; representative examples:\n- N04 AC: \"every `story` node carries an Acceptance-criteria block (checked by `bin/check-plan-framing`)\"; \"every deliverable carries `serves_kr`, every node `type` (N02 walk-script succeeds).\"\n- N07 success metric: \"2/2 trial initiatives pass `bin/check-plan-framing` at 0 re-prompts; <2/2 or any re-prompt falsifies the bet.\"\n- N08 AC: \"`eval/delivery-shape-triggers.md` holds 8–10 labelled phrasings; `bin/eval-triggers` exits 0 only on 0 collisions in either direction.\"\n- N09: ktlo carve-out (roadmap A5) — README skill-table row + `using-this-pack` flowchart entry; no outcome hypothesis.\n\n## Tools (load schemas at execution)\n\n- `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__create_issue_label` — create `skill:product-spike`, `skill:skill-creator` first (capture IDs).\n- `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__save_milestone` — create D2, D3 in the project (capture IDs).\n- `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__save_issue` — create the 6 issues; set project, milestone, labels, title, description, state=Todo. ## Approved Plan (edited by user):
# Create outstanding deliverables (D2, D3) in Linear

## Context

The initiative **"Top-down delivery planning — framing baked in, foundational work never silent"** (project `8b6ce9b1`, team ABA) has finished its walking-skeleton spike (D1 — issues ABA-281/282/283/284, all Done). That cleared **KR1** (the foundation): the plan-artefact contract, the worked file-set, and the walk-script all exist and the manifest matches the hand-count (3 milestones / 9 issues / 19 sub-issues).

The two outstanding deliverables — **D2 (the skill itself, → KR2)** and **D3 (trigger eval + discoverability, → KR3)** — exist only as nodes in the worked plan at `examples/delivery-plans/top-down-delivery-planning/`. This task creates their Linear milestones + issues so they can be pulled into the next cycle.

The user asked to drive creation off the manifest where possible, and to label each issue with (a) reviewer complexity, (b) the Claude model to execute it, (c) the specific skill if one applies.

## What's amiss (manifest can't do it all on its own)

1. **No model/review/skill tags in the artefacts.** Node front-matter carries `type`, `serves_kr`, `skeleton`, `acceptance`. The manifest propagates only `type` + `serves_kr`. → The three requested labels are **derived by judgment below**, not read off the manifest.
2. **Label vocabulary** — all required labels now exist. `model:{opus,sonnet,haiku}`, `review:high`, `repo:agent-skills-shaper`, `skill:source-driven-development`, and (created by Anton during review) `skill:product-spike` + `skill:skill-creator`. No label creation needed. (Review stays binary by decision — no `review:medium/low`.)
3. **Tasks ≠ sub-issues in practice.** Manifest maps task→sub-issue, but D1 ran tasks as in-body checklists (ABA-282 has 0 sub-issues).

## Decisions (incl. user answers)

- **Review scale: binary** (user). Apply `review:high` only where careful review is genuinely needed; leave the rest unlabelled. No new review labels.
- **Tasks: in-body checklist, node = issue** (reasoned, user wants this codified). The issue is the lifecycle unit (`rules/linear-workflow.md`: review→commit→Done is per-issue); a node's completion criterion is the issue's Done; tasks are the steps toward it. Result: **6 issues**, each with a `- [ ]` checklist. No sub-issues. (Proposed rule text in the last section, for later codification — not edited now.)
- **Skill labels: reuse existing.** `skill:product-spike` and `skill:skill-creator` were created by Anton during plan review; `skill:source-driven-development` already existed. No label creation step.
- **Milestones: create D2 and D3** (manifest models deliverable→milestone; D1 was not milestoned, that's fine — this dogfoods the contract). Do not retro-milestone D1.
- **Cycle: leave unassigned.** Cycle 2 closes today; next cycle isn't created yet. These get pulled in at next planning (status update already says "carry into the next cycle"). Create in **Todo** state.
- **Dependencies: prose "Blocked by …" in the description**, referencing prior issue identifiers (matches D1; native blocking relations aren't needed).

## Label assignment matrix (the core judgment)

| Node | Title | Milestone | KR | model | review | skill | repo |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| N04 | Decompose initiative → deliverables → nodes → tasks, AC by default | D2 | KR2 | `model:opus` | `review:high` | `skill:source-driven-development` | `repo:agent-skills-shaper` |
| N05 | Foundational-work prompt folded into the walking skeleton | D2 | KR2 | `model:opus` | `review:high` | `skill:source-driven-development` | `repo:agent-skills-shaper` |
| N06 | Rule A1 design-doc branch + up/down delegation | D2 | KR2 | `model:opus` | `review:high` | `skill:source-driven-development` | `repo:agent-skills-shaper` |
| N07 | Self-trial against 2 already-shaped initiatives | D2 | KR2 | `model:opus` | `review:high` | `skill:product-spike` | `repo:agent-skills-shaper` |
| N08 | Trigger-eval set + harness, 0 collisions | D3 | KR3 | `model:sonnet` | — | `skill:skill-creator` | `repo:agent-skills-shaper` |
| N09 | Register delivery-shape in README + using-this-pack flowchart | D3 | KR3 | `model:haiku` | — | — | `repo:agent-skills-shaper` |

**Rationale.** N04–N06 author the skill's spine (decomposition + AC-by-default, foundational-work fold, delegation/Rule-A1 branch); prose nuance and no-fabrication grounding against `docs/skill-anatomy.md` + principle IDs → opus + source-driven-development; reviewer must read carefully → high. N07 is the KR2 verdict (does the bet hold?) — evaluation judgment → opus + product-spike discipline; reviewer scrutinises the finding → high. N08 is the KR3 brake; description-tuning + a deterministic trigger eval (`bin/eval-triggers` exits 0 on 0 collisions) is exactly skill-creator's domain → sonnet, iterating against the harness; the deterministic gate means no review:high. N09 is a ktlo docs row + flowchart line → haiku, self-verifying, unlabelled for review/skill. (D1 calibration was opus + review:high across the board, consistent with N04–N07.)

## Out of scope / follow-ups (note, don't do now)

- **Codify the task-representation rule.** Proposed text, for `rules/linear-workflow.md` (lifecycle) and `docs/delivery-shape-contract.md` (conversion):
  > A delivery-plan **node maps to one Linear issue** — the issue is the unit that runs the full Done-cycle (review → commit+push → summary comment → Done), and the node's completion criterion is that issue's Done. A node's **leaf tasks map to an in-body `- [ ]` checklist**, not to sub-issues, because they share the node's single Done-cycle. Create a **sub-issue only when a leaf needs its own Done-cycle** (its own acceptance criterion / own commit) — and treat that need as the signal to re-model the leaf as a node and its parent as a capability. Supersedes the literal task→sub-issue line in the manifest schema.

14	    hypothesis or a measurable success criterion. Registration is housekeeping.
15	delegates_to: ops slot (no initiative outcome framing; KTLO carve-out)
16	---
17	
18	# N09 — Register delivery-shape in README + using-this-pack flowchart
19	
20	**Type:** `ktlo`. This is the one node in the plan that carries **no completion criterion** — the
21	roadmap A5 carve-out: bug fixes, compliance items, and minor maintenance skip outcome framing on
22	purpose, so the schema does not inflate housekeeping into a bet. It routes to the **ops slot**,
23	not an initiative outcome.
24	
25	> Documenting this node as KTLO is itself part of the contract test: the schema must be able to
26	> carry a node that *has no acceptance criterion* without breaking the walk.
27	
28	## Maintenance statement
29	
30	Add a `delivery-shape` row to the README skill table and a routing entry to the
31	`using-this-pack` flowchart so the skill is discoverable. No metric to move.
32	
33	## Tasks

It is the **acceptance node** — its finding is D2's Done condition.\n\n**Serves:** D2 → KR2 (the bet). `acceptance: true`, `verifies_parent: D2`.\n\n**Hypothesis**\ndelivery-shape, fed only an initiative's goal + KRs as text, emits a plan that carries acceptance criteria on every story node and at least one foundational / walking-skeleton task — at zero framing re-prompts.\n\n**Success metric**\n2/2 trial initiatives pass `bin/check-plan-framing` (AC on every story + ≥1 skeleton task) at 0 re-prompts. < 2/2, or any re-prompt, falsifies the bet. Trial plans live under `examples/delivery-plans/_trials/`; the trial note records re-prompt count per trial.\n\n**Tasks**\n\n- [ ] `skeleton` — Run delivery-shape against one already-shaped initiative; record AC-coverage, foundational-task presence, and re-prompt count in a trial note.\n- [ ] `acceptance` — Repeat against a second initiative; evaluate aggregate result against the 2/2-at-0-re-prompts success metric; write the finding (confirmed / falsified) — this finding is D2's Done condition.\n\n**Blocked by:** N04 (<issue id=\"c078b8ed-6e94-4a15-9d23-96ea345b7528\">ABA-285</issue>), N05 (<issue id=\"556dd8dc-1bb2-400c-94fa-9eae4559a183\">ABA-286</issue>), N06 (<issue id=\"8427b338-e1d9-4a80-8842-400777215201\">ABA-287</issue>) — all implementation stories must land before the trial.\n**Delegates to:** `product-spike` (experiment discipline — hypothesis, success metric, written finding).\n**Source:** `examples/delivery-plans/top-down-delivery-planning/D2-delivery-shape-skill-framing-baked-in/N07-self-trial-framing-baked-in.md`","projectMilestone":{"id":"1ea3966d-6d41-4ce2-8363-965316cbaa37","name":"D2 — The delivery-shape skill, framing baked in"},"priority":{"value":0,"name":"No priority"},"url":"https://linear.app/ababushkin/issue/ABA-288/self-trial-against-2-already-shaped-initiatives-acceptance-closes-d2","gitBranchName":"anton/aba-288-self-trial-against-2-already-shaped-initiatives-acceptance","createdAt":"2026-05-25T13:00:56.972Z","updatedAt":"2026-05-25T13:00:58.679Z","archivedAt":null,"completedAt":null,"startedAt":null,"canceledAt":null,"dueDate":null,"slaStartedAt":null,"slaMediumRiskAt":null,"slaHighRiskAt":null,"slaBreachesAt":null,"slaType":"all","status":"Todo","statusType":"unstarted","labels":["model:opus","review:high","skill:product-spike","repo:agent-skills-shaper"],"attachments":[],"documents":[],"createdBy":"Anton Babushkin","createdById":"720d0535-dfc6-465f-8111-d1a3e6eab786","project":"Top-down delivery planning — framing baked in, foundational work never silent","projectId":"8b6ce9b1-6b87-4d3b-97fc-2b1ffe17a608","team":"Personal","teamId":"c20f56da-f121-4653-bf6c-dc1c8aac21c1"}

---

## Does `planning-and-task-breakdown` still have a job? Yes — and your mental model is intact.

Your pipeline still holds. `delivery-shape` doesn't replace `planning-and-task-breakdown` (PTB) — it slots in *above* it as the "design/plan delivery" layer you already had in mind:

```
idea-triage → initiative-shape → [design-doc if Rule A1] → delivery-shape → planning-and-task-breakdown → build
  (intake)     (shape the bet)      (one-way doors)        (top-down plan)      (per-node task list)
```

**The split is orchestrator vs. engine.** This was the exact question the gate ADR (decision A) settled. `delivery-shape` *owns* the top-down structure — decomposing a committed initiative into deliverables → nodes, the `serves_kr` outcome spine at every layer, node-type selection, the Rule A1 design-doc branch, and the walkable file-set → tracker manifest. It does **not** own the detailed task breakdown of any single node. For that, **it delegates down to PTB** — that's literally written into N04/N05/N06's `delegates_to: planning-and-task-breakdown`.

So PTB ends up with *two* roles, and arguably gets used **more**, not less:

1. **As the engine `delivery-shape` calls** — once per node. Turning one node into a correctly-ordered, individually-verifiable task sequence (walking-skeleton-first, no pre-skeleton setup task, dependencies) is genuinely hard work, and `delivery-shape` deliberately doesn't re-implement it. N05 even *delegates the walking-skeleton-first rule itself* to PTB.
2. **Standalone, for the small case** — you have one spec or one well-defined chunk, no initiative, no deliverable hierarchy. You skip straight to PTB. No `delivery-shape` needed.

**Why not just fold PTB into the new skill?** The ADR's reasoning: PTB has a clean single-input/single-output contract ("a chunk of work → an ordered, verifiable task list"). `delivery-shape` layers on top with low coupling. Re-authoring PTB's logic inside `delivery-shape` would (a) duplicate it, guaranteeing drift (agentic P7 — "memory lives in artefacts, don't re-author"), and (b) make `delivery-shape` two skills wearing one frontmatter. Keeping them separate keeps each responsibility in one place.

**The one real risk is exactly the one you're feeling — overlap.** Both skills "decompose into tasks," so a human (or the model) could reach for the wrong one. That's precisely why **KR3 / D3 (ABA-289) exists**: a trigger eval that fails unless `delivery-shape` *never* fires on a "break this into tasks" prompt and PTB *never* fires on a "plan the whole delivery of this initiative" prompt. The boundary is being made mechanically sharp, not left to vibes.

So: nothing is being retired. `delivery-shape` is the missing middle layer; PTB stays as the per-node workhorse beneath it and the go-to for small standalone breakdowns.

> DEVELOPER

right so will the /planning-and-task-breakdown skill be automatically fired from /delivery-shape ?

> AGENT

Short answer: **no — not in a programmatic "event fires a function" sense.** That's worth being precise about, because it changes how you design D2.

**Skills aren't functions that call each other.** In Claude Code a skill is a Markdown instruction set that gets loaded into context when invoked (via the Skill tool). There's no hook that auto-fires PTB when `delivery-shape` runs. So "delegation" here doesn't mean a call-stack — it means `delivery-shape`'s authored workflow will contain an explicit step that tells the agent *"now invoke `planning-and-task-breakdown` for this node,"* and the agent follows it by invoking the PTB skill. From your seat that *looks* automatic — you run `/delivery-shape`, and at the per-node step it pulls in PTB — but the mechanism is "the model obeys an instruction the skill author wrote," not a wired trigger.

**And it doesn't exist yet.** This wiring is precisely what **N06 / ABA-287** ("Rule A1 branch + up/down delegation") builds. So the honest status is: *it'll fire from delivery-shape if and only if we author it to.*

**The design choice you actually need to make (before N06):** *when* does PTB fire?

- **In-session** — `delivery-shape` invokes PTB on every node during the same run, producing one big, fully-expanded plan up front.
- **Deferred, per-issue (my recommendation)** — `delivery-shape` emits only the node hierarchy to the tracker (milestones + issues, exactly like the six we just created). PTB then fires *per issue, at build time*, when someone picks up ABA-285 to actually work it.

I'd go deferred, for three reasons that are already principles in your pack:
- **Small batches / certainty decays with horizon** — expanding all six nodes into fine-grained task sequences *now* front-loads detail that'll be stale by the time you reach N08. Most of it will change.
- **It matches the lifecycle you already run** — `rules/linear-workflow.md`'s "on start of an issue" step is the natural moment to break that one issue into its build tasks.
- **It's what we just did** — the issues carry coarse node-level checklists (delivery-shape's output); PTB's job is to expand *one* node into its ordered, verifiable build steps when it's time to build it. No redundancy between the two layers.

Net: `delivery-shape` = decompose top-down and hand off; PTB = expand one node on demand. The hand-off is an authored workflow step, fired by the agent, not an automatic trigger — and deferring it to build-time keeps the layers from duplicating each other.

Want me to record this (deferred per-issue invocation) as a decision note on **ABA-287** so it's pinned before the skill gets authored? It's the kind of thing that'll otherwise get re-litigated mid-build.

> DEVELOPER

i'm ok with it to happen right before build, but that means that needs to be clearly encapsulated in the task so that when it gets picked up, the agent knows what to do

> AGENT

Agreed — and the robust place for it is *in each issue*, because an agent picking up a Linear issue reads that issue's description, not a separate rule file. Let me generalize your point slightly: each issue should name **its own** delegate at pickup (PTB for the build stories; product-spike / skill-creator for the others; nothing for the ktlo), since not every node breaks down through PTB. I'll add a visible "On pickup" callout to all six.

Then build under `source-driven-development` (the `skill:` label).\n\n**Story**\nAs an agent invoking delivery-shape, I want the skill to branch on the Rule A1 design-doc trigger and to delegate up to `initiative-shape` and down to `planning-and-task-breakdown`, so that it never re-authors sub-skill gates (which guarantees drift, agentic P7) and a design-doc-worthy deliverable gets a design doc before tasks.\n\n**Acceptance criteria** (this issue's Done)\n- Done when: a deliverable that meets a Rule A1 trigger routes to `design-doc` before task breakdown; one that does not proceeds on goal + deliverables. Both branches exercised in a test plan.\n- Done when: the skill cites `initiative-shape` (up) and `planning-and-task-breakdown` (down) by current name and contains no copied gate text from either — checked by grep against the two SKILL.md files.\n\n**Tasks** (node-level scope — expand via PTB at pickup)\n- [ ] Implement the Rule A1 branch (trigger met → delegate to design-doc; else proceed).\n- [ ] Wire up delegation: consume `initiative-shape` output; hand each node to `planning-and-task-breakdown`.\n\n**Blocked by:** N04 (ABA-285).\n**Delegates to:** `design-doc` (Rule A1 trigger); `initiative-shape` (upstream goal+KRs); `planning-and-task-breakdown` (downstream tasks).\n**Source:** `examples/delivery-plans/top-down-delivery-planning/D2-delivery-shape-skill-framing-baked-in/N06-rule-a1-branch-and-delegation.md`"
}

{
  "issueId": "ABA-287",
  "body": "**Design decision — deferred, build-time delegation (pinned so it isn't re-litigated mid-build).**\n\nDelegation does **not** fire during the `delivery-shape` run. `delivery-shape` emits the deliverable→node hierarchy to the tracker (milestones + issues) and stops. The delegated skill — `planning-and-task-breakdown` for build stories, `product-spike`/`skill-creator` for the others — fires **at build time, per issue, when the issue is picked up**. Rationale: small batches + certainty-decays-with-horizon (don't expand all nodes' fine tasks up front; most will change), and it matches the `linear-workflow` \"on start of an issue\" step.\n\nThere is no programmatic auto-trigger between skills (skills are instruction sets, not functions). \"Fires automatically\" only means the emitted issue **carries an explicit on-pickup instruction** that the picking-up agent follows.\n\n**Therefore this node (N06) and the emitter (N04) must bake the hand-off into the artefact, not leave it to the agent's memory (agentic P7):**\n- Every emitted issue carries an `▶ On pickup` line naming the skill from its `delegates_to` to invoke *before* building (PTB for stories; the node's named delegate otherwise; none for ktlo).\n- That line is part of `delivery-shape`'s emitted-issue template — not hand-added per issue.\n\nThe six already-created D2/D3 issues (ABA-285…290) have this line added by hand as the worked example of the target output."
}
```

> AGENT

Done. Here's where it landed:

**On all six issues** — a visible `▶ On pickup` callout at the top of the description, naming the right delegate so the picking-up agent knows exactly what to do before building:
- **ABA-285/286/287** (build stories) → invoke `planning-and-task-breakdown` to expand the node into ordered, skeleton-first build tasks, *then* build under `source-driven-development`. The checklist is explicitly flagged as node-level scope, not the build sequence.
- **ABA-288** (experiment) → run under `product-spike`; PTB doesn't apply.
- **ABA-289** (eval) → invoke `skill-creator`.
- **ABA-290** (ktlo) → no breakdown step; just do the one task.

**On ABA-287** — pinned the design decision as a comment, because hand-adding this line to six issues isn't the real fix. The durable fix is that `delivery-shape`'s **emitted-issue template** must carry the `On pickup` line automatically (derived from each node's `delegates_to`) — that's now a written constraint on N06/N04, so whoever authors the skill bakes it in rather than rediscovering it. The six issues stand as the worked example of the target output. This is exactly the initiative's own thesis applied to itself: the hand-off is baked into the artefact, not left to the next agent's memory.

Net: the deferred, build-time delegation you approved is now encapsulated where an agent will actually see it (the issue), and the rule behind it is captured where it'll get implemented (the skill).

One loose end still untouched from earlier: the uncommitted `rules/linear-workflow.md` edit ("Run using a sub-agent"). Want me to commit that as an ops tweak, or leave it?

> DEVELOPER

i've already commited that change
how are we going to make sure that this rule is encoded correctly for the future when ever a new project is shaped?

> AGENT

Good. The honest answer: **don't rely on the `delivery-shape` skill (or any agent) "remembering" to add the line.** That's the exact thing your own `eng-principles-universal` Rule A5 rejects — *"a target that isn't continuously verified will drift; 'we'll watch out for that' is not a plan."* Hand-adding it to six issues, or even baking it into the skill's prose, is the weak version. You need it encoded in two places — the producer and the consumer — with a check that fails loudly.

## Where the rule has to live

**1. Consumer side — `rules/linear-workflow.md` (the keystone).**
This is the strongest guarantee because that file is **imported into your global CLAUDE.md** — it's loaded into *every* session, automatically. If the "on start of an issue" step says *"honor the issue's delegate before building,"* then every agent picking up any issue has the rule in context, regardless of whether the issue's own description remembered the callout. Proposed addition to the **On start** list:

> - If the issue carries a `Delegates to` / `▶ On pickup` instruction, invoke that skill first to expand the node into build tasks before writing code (ktlo issues carry no breakdown step).

**2. Producer side — `docs/delivery-shape-contract.md` (the spec).**
The issue schema should state that each emitted issue carries an `On pickup` instruction **derived from the node's `delegates_to`** — not optional, not hand-written. This is what the skill author (when D2 is built) implements against. Encoding it in the contract is what makes "for the future" true rather than aspirational.

**3. Enforcement — `bin/check-plan-framing` (the fitness function).**
The spike already built `check-plan-framing` to assert framing (AC on every story + ≥1 skeleton task). Extend it: **fail unless every node's `delegates_to` is surfaced as an on-pickup instruction.** That's the seatbelt — a delivery plan that emits an issue without its delegate line fails the check, so it can't silently regress. This folds into **N04 (ABA-285)** as a third acceptance criterion.

## So, concretely

- **#1 and #2 are encodings I can do now** — they're prose rules, directly answering "make sure it's encoded for the future."
- **#3 is built when N04 (ABA-285) is implemented** — but it needs to be in N04's AC, which it currently isn't. Right now the rule only lives in my comment on ABA-287, which is too weak.

1	# delivery-shape — plan-artefact contract
2	
3	The schema for a **delivery plan**: the set of cross-referenced markdown files that turn one
4	committed initiative into an ordered, verifiable delivery hierarchy — deliverable → node → task —
5	that a human reads top-down and a deterministic reader walks bottom-up into a tracker manifest.
6	
7	This contract was **read off a worked example**, not designed in the abstract:
8	[`examples/delivery-plans/top-down-delivery-planning/`](../examples/delivery-plans/top-down-delivery-planning/).
9	Read that example alongside this spec — every rule here points at a node that demonstrates it.
10	
11	> **Tool-agnostic by rule.** This contract names no tracker, language, or runtime. It describes
12	> *file-set structure* and *tracker-artefact classes* (milestone-class / issue-class /
13	> sub-issue-class). Binding those classes to a concrete tracker is the **adapter's** job and lives
14	> in another repo. Emitted plans (the example) may name concrete tools; this contract may not.
15	
16	---
17	
18	## The three layers
19	
20	| Layer | Lives as | Tracker-artefact class | Completion criterion |
21	|-------|----------|------------------------|----------------------|
22	| **Deliverable** | a directory `D*/` with `_deliverable.md` | milestone-class | its served KR is observed |
23	| **Node** | a file `N*.md` | issue-class | **type-dependent** (see vocabulary) |
24	| **Task** | a `- [ ]` / `- [x]` line inside a node | sub-issue-class | the checkbox + its description |
25	
26	A node is **polymorphic**: it is not always a user story. It carries whichever completion
27	criterion its work calls for — a story carries acceptance criteria, a spike carries a decision and
28	a stop condition, a capability carries its own spec, and so on. This is the central claim the
29	example exists to prove. (A capability is one of these node types, not a separate structural
30	layer — see *Design notes* for why the earlier four-layer model was collapsed.)
31	
32	---
33	
34	## Schema element 1 — Directory layout
35	
36	Directory nesting **is** the hierarchy. Depth encodes layer; nothing else needs to:
37	
38	```
39	<initiative-slug>/
40	├── README.md                  initiative root: goal + KRs + how-to-read + tree + hand-count manifest
41	├── D<n>-<slug>/               deliverable      → milestone-class
42	│   ├── _deliverable.md        the deliverable's own front-matter + statement
43	│   ├── N<nn>-<slug>.md        node             → issue-class
44	│   └── …
45	└── …
46	```
47	
48	Rules:
49	
50	- **Numeric prefixes** (`D1`, `N03`) give a deterministic reading and walking order. Pad node
51	  numbers (`N01`) so lexical sort = intended sort past nine nodes.
52	- **Layer-header files** are underscore-prefixed (`_deliverable.md`) so they sort
53	  first within their directory and are unambiguously the layer object, not a node.
54	- **Node files** are the only `N*.md` files; one node per file.
55	- The **root `README.md`** is the single top-down entry point: it carries the goal + KRs verbatim
56	  (read top-down starts at the outcome, not at task 1), the rendered tree, and the **hand-count
57	  manifest** the walk-script must reproduce.
58	
59	## Schema element 2 — Cross-reference convention
60	
61	**Relative paths + directory hierarchy + front-matter back-links.** Three reinforcing signals, no
62	hand-maintained index:
63	
64	- **Down** — a parent links to each child by relative path (`_deliverable.md` lists its
65	  nodes; `README.md` renders the whole tree).
66	- **Up** — every file's front-matter carries `parent:` (the enclosing layer) so a reader that
67	  starts at any node can climb without scanning directories.
68	- **To the bet** — every deliverable and node carries `serves_kr:` naming the KR it serves, so the
69	  outcome trace is explicit at every layer, not inferred from position.
70	
71	The **manifest is derived, never authored**: a walk-script reconstructs milestone/issue/sub-issue
72	counts from the directory structure + front-matter. The hand-count in the README exists only as
73	the oracle the derived manifest is checked against. (A hand-maintained manifest file was
74	considered and rejected — it is a second source of truth that drifts; see *Design notes*.)
75	
76	## Schema element 3 — Per-node tags (front-matter)
77	
78	Every layer file opens with YAML front-matter. The load-bearing tags:
79	
80	```yaml
81	layer:        deliverable | node                   # which layer this file is
82	id:           D1 | N03                              # stable within the plan
83	type:         <node-type>                          # NODE FILES ONLY — the polymorphic node discriminator (see vocabulary); `capability` is one of these types
84	title:        <one line>
85	parent:       <relative path or id of enclosing layer>
86	serves_kr:    KR<n>                                # deliverable↔KR and node↔KR trace
87	maps_to:      <tracker-artefact class>             # milestone/issue/sub-issue class
88	skeleton:     true | (absent)                      # NODE-LEVEL: is this the walking-skeleton node?
89	acceptance:   true | (absent)                      # NODE-LEVEL: does this node verify its parent's cross-seam criterion?
90	external_window: <external constraint> | none      # see constraints — never effort-in-days
91	completion:
92	  form:       <completion-criterion form>          # the type-appropriate form (see vocabulary)
93	  criterion:  <the observable criterion, by form>
94	  verifies_parent: <parent-layer-id> | (absent)   # when acceptance: true — which parent's criterion this node closes
95	delegates_to: <rule or skill that owns this node type's discipline>
96	```
97	
98	The five tags the contract is required to pin down:
99	
100	1. **node `type`** — selects the completion-criterion form and the delegation target (vocabulary below).
101	2. **deliverable↔KR** — `serves_kr:` on each `_deliverable.md` (and echoed on nodes). Every
102	   deliverable serves exactly one KR; this is the outcome spine.
103	3. **completion-criterion** — `completion.form` + `completion.criterion`, shaped by `type`.
104	4. **task↔skeleton flag** — at the **task** level, the walking-skeleton/foundational task is marked
105	   with a leading `` `skeleton` `` tag inside its checklist line. At the **node** level, the node
106	   that *is* the initiative's walking skeleton carries `skeleton: true` in front-matter. Foundational
107	   / toolchain work is **folded into** the skeleton task's description — never a silent setup task
108	   before it (mirrors `planning-and-task-breakdown`'s walking-skeleton-first rule). There is no
109	   `foundational` node type: foundational work is a property of the skeleton **task**, not a node.
110	
111	5. **task↔acceptance flag** — at the **task** level, the aggregate/cross-seam-verification task is
112	   marked with a leading `` `acceptance` `` tag inside its checklist line, mirroring `` `skeleton` ``
113	   exactly. At the **node** level, a node that verifies its **parent's** emergent or cross-seam
114	   completion criterion carries `acceptance: true` in front-matter plus
115	   `completion.verifies_parent: <parent-id>` naming which parent layer it closes. `skeleton` opens
116	   the end-to-end path; `acceptance` closes it.
117	
118	   **Grounding rule (reducibility-gated):** an `acceptance` node or task exists **only when the
119	   parent's "Done when" is irreducible to ∀child.done** — a KR moving, a journey crossing child
120	   seams, an integration no single child owns. When the criterion is the sum of its children, there
121	   is no acceptance node — the parent closing is the verification. (Same reducibility test used to
122	   decide when `capability` is a node vs absorbed prose, applied to the completion axis instead of
123	   the structure axis.) An acceptance node **never re-runs child criteria** — that is the redundancy
124	   guard.
125	
126	   **Linear mapping:** the `acceptance` flag maps to a Linear label (orthogonal to structure, like
127	   `skeleton`). Close the acceptance node **last**: this makes the milestone reaching 100% coincide
128	   with aggregate verification passing — no special field or tooling required. See `N07` for the
129	   worked example.
130	
131	Task lines are the sub-issue layer. A task is `- [ ] ` or `- [x] `; an optional leading code-span
132	flags it. `` `skeleton` `` marks the walking-skeleton/foundational task; `` `acceptance` `` marks
133	the aggregate-verification task (flag 5 above). Examples:
134	`` - [ ] `skeleton` — walk the tree, read front-matter, print counts (toolchain folded in) ``.
135	`` - [ ] `acceptance` — evaluate aggregate result; write finding (confirmed / falsified) ``.
136	
137	---
138	
139	## Node-type vocabulary
140	
141	`delivery-shape` **selects** the node type and **delegates** its discipline to the rule or skill
142	that owns it. It does **not** re-author SLO targets, ADR structure, spike protocol, or postmortem
143	format — that guarantees drift (agentic P7). Each type below names its completion-criterion form
144	and its delegation target.
145	
146	### Grounded — exercised by the worked example
147	
148	Each row cites the example node that demonstrates it. These are **node `type`s** — the polymorphic
149	discriminator carried on `N*.md` files. A delegation target is either a **Shaper rule** (always
150	present in this repo) or a **skill** — skills marked *(skill where available)* are not yet
151	Shaper-native, so the rule beside them is the durable fallback a future agent can rely on.
152	
153	| `type` | Completion-criterion form | Delegates to | Demonstrated by |
154	|--------|---------------------------|--------------|-----------------|
155	| `spike` | decision + stop condition | `eng-principles-universal.md` Rule C5 (time-box; written decision at the box) | `N01` |
156	| `story` | acceptance criteria, in a grounded story form | `planning-and-task-breakdown` (per-node task breakdown) | `N02`, `N04`, `N05`, `N06`, `N08` |
157	| `adr` | an accepted decision record (Context / Decision / Consequences) | `eng-principles-universal.md` Rule A3 (ADR) + D3 (living ADRs); `documentation-and-adrs` *(skill where available)* | `N03` |
158	| `experiment` | hypothesis + success metric (confirmed / falsified) | `product-spike` (experiment discipline) | `N07` |
159	| `ktlo` | **none** — carve-out | ops slot (no outcome framing; roadmap A5) | `N09` |
160	
161	`capability` **is** a node `type` — a spec-shaped node broken into task-slices, completion form
162	`the-capability-spec`. This initiative exercises none: every capability in the worked example was
163	reducible to its child nodes and was absorbed into deliverable prose (see *Design notes*), so
164	`capability` sits in the to-fill appendix below rather than the grounded table. It grounds the
165	moment a capability carries a spec *not* reducible to its children — at which point it becomes a
166	node, a peer of the `story` / `spike` / … nodes under its deliverable.
167	
168	### To-fill appendix — not exercised by this initiative
169	
170	These types are **named, not fabricated**: this methodology-pack initiative has no runtime SLO, no
171	production migration, no incident, no deprecation, and no external compliance obligation, so the
172	worked example contains none. `capability` is here for a different reason — not domain absence but
173	**structural reducibility** (every capability collapsed into its child nodes, as noted above). The
174	completion-criterion form and delegation target are recorded so the vocabulary is ready when an
175	initiative that genuinely exercises them is shaped — at which point the row moves up to *Grounded*
176	with a citation.
177	
178	| `type` | Completion-criterion form | Delegates to | Ground when… |
179	|--------|---------------------------|--------------|--------------|
180	| `capability` | `the-capability-spec` | `planning-and-task-breakdown` (slice the capability spec into tasks) | a capability carries a spec not reducible to its child nodes |
181	| `slo` | numeric target (latency/availability/error-budget) | `eng-principles-universal.md` Rule A4 (measurable NFR) + A5 (fitness function) | a production/observability initiative is planned |
182	| `migration` | rollback plan **per phase** + cutover criterion | `eng-principles-universal.md` Rule A6 (rollback); `deprecation-and-migration` *(skill where available)* | a data/system migration is planned |
183	| `deprecation` | removal criterion + external **notice period** | `eng-principles-universal.md` Rule A6 (rollback); `deprecation-and-migration` *(skill where available)* | an API/feature sunset is planned |
184	| `incident` | blameless postmortem (contributing factors, remediations) | `eng-principles-universal.md` Rule D1 | the plan absorbs incident-driven remediation |
185	| `compliance` | obligation met by an external **deadline** | ops slot / Rule A5 carve-out | a legal/partner obligation enters the plan |
186	
187	> **Do not invent a node of an appendix type to "cover" it.** A plan exercises a type only when the
188	> initiative's real work calls for it. Padding the example with a fake SLO would make the schema
189	> look more proven than it is — the exact failure the grounding rule prevents.
190	
191	### Completion-criterion forms (the menu)
192	
193	`acceptance-criteria` · `decision+stop-condition` · `decision-record` · `hypothesis+success-metric`
194	· `numeric-target` · `rollback-per-phase` · `the-capability-spec` · `none` (carve-out). A node's
195	`completion.form` is one of these, fixed by its `type`.
196	
197	---
198	
199	## Constraints on the contract
200	
201	- **Grounded story form only.** A `story` node uses a recognised template — Cohn
202	  ("As a [role], I want [capability], so that [benefit]") or a job story
203	  ("When [situation], I want to [motivation], so I can [outcome]"). A custom "so… so…" chain is
204	  not permitted: it hides the role and the benefit, the two things acceptance criteria are checked
205	  against. See `N02`/`N04`/`N05`/`N06`/`N08` for the Cohn form in use.
206	- **External windows, never effort-in-days.** The optional `external_window` field carries *only*
207	  genuine external constraints — a deprecation notice period, a legal or compliance deadline, a
208	  canary soak time, an embargo. It must **never** carry an effort or duration estimate in
209	  days/weeks (agentic P8: effort is measured in slices and gates, not calendar time). Every node in
210	  the worked example is `external_window: none` because this initiative has no external constraint;
211	  the appendix types (`deprecation`, `compliance`, `slo` soak) are where the field goes non-`none`.
212	  Sizing lives in the **appetite** (issue count = node count) and in **task granularity**, not in a
213	  per-node time field.
214	- **Tool-agnostic templates.** This contract and any template it ships name no tracker, language, or
215	  runtime. An emitted plan (the example) may name concrete tools in its prose and in `maps_to`
216	  values; the contract describes only the artefact classes those tools implement.
217	- **Select and delegate; never re-author.** `delivery-shape` picks the node `type` and points
218	  `delegates_to` at the owning rule/skill. The discipline of each type lives at its source. If a
219	  node's completion criterion starts duplicating Rule C5 or the ADR template inline, that is drift —
220	  link instead.
221	
222	---
223	
224	## Walkability contract (what a deterministic reader may rely on)
225	
226	This is the half of the kill condition the gate issue tests with an actual walk-script. A reader
227	may assume, without parsing prose:
228	
229	1. **Layers are directory depth.** `D*/` = deliverable, `D*/N*.md` = node.
230	2. **Front-matter is the source of structured truth.** Every layer file opens with a `---` YAML
231	   block carrying at least `layer`, `id`, and (for nodes) `type` + `completion.form`.
232	3. **The manifest is countable.** milestones = `D*` directories; issues = `N*.md` files;
233	   sub-issues = `- [ ]`/`- [x]` lines inside node files.
234	4. **The oracle is the README.** The derived manifest is correct iff its counts equal the
235	   hand-count manifest in the root README; a mismatch means drift or a non-deterministic layout.
236	
237	If a reader cannot satisfy 1–4 mechanically, the standalone-markdown premise has failed and the
238	seam must be re-shaped before the skill is built (the initiative kill condition).
239	
240	## Design notes / alternatives considered
241	
242	- **File-per-node vs section-per-node.** Chosen: one file per node, structure carried by directory
243	  depth + front-matter. Rejected: nodes as `##` sections inside the `_deliverable.md` file with
244	  fenced metadata blocks — fewer files, but it forces the reader to parse headings and extract fenced
245	  blocks, which is more fragile than reading front-matter from a known file path. If a future
246	  walk-script finds front-matter parsing insufficient, section-per-node is the documented fallback —
247	  but the burden is on showing front-matter failed first.
248	- **Derived vs hand-maintained manifest.** Chosen: derive the manifest from structure; keep only a
249	  hand-count *oracle* in the README. Rejected: a checked-in manifest file enumerating every
250	  milestone/issue/sub-issue — a second source of truth that drifts from the files it indexes.
251	- **Four structural layers vs three.** Chosen: three — deliverable → node → task, mapping 1:1 to
252	  milestone / issue / sub-issue. Rejected: a fourth `capability` layer mapped to a label. A label is
253	  *stateless* (name + colour, no done-state, no target), yet a capability asserts a real completion
254	  criterion — a stateful claim a label can't hold or check; and the mapping was asymmetric
255	  (deliverables, the same kind of object, get a stateful milestone). The fix folds `capability` into
256	  the polymorphic node vocabulary as a node *type* (completion form `the-capability-spec`): a
257	  capability that carries its own irreducible spec is a node, broken into task-slices, sitting beside
258	  the `story` / `spike` / … nodes under its deliverable. Labels remain available for *orthogonal*
259	  tagging (theme / area), just not as a hierarchy layer. In this worked example every capability was
260	  reducible to its child nodes, so all five were absorbed into deliverable prose and no
261	  `capability`-type node appears — it grounds when one carries a spec its children don't.
262

1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
2	"""walk-delivery-plan — walk a delivery-plan file-set into a tracker manifest.
3	
4	Reads a delivery plan laid out per `docs/delivery-shape-contract.md`
5	(deliverable directories → node files → task checklist lines) and emits a
6	tracker-shaped manifest: milestone-class ← deliverables, issue-class ← nodes,
7	sub-issue-class ← tasks. The manifest is *derived* from directory structure +
8	front-matter, never authored.
9	
10	The script is the mechanical-conversion half of the delivery-shape spike's kill
11	gate. It is deliberately dependency-free (stdlib only, no YAML library): the
12	front-matter the contract pins down is a small fixed set of keys, and a targeted
13	reader for those keys is more boring — and more honest about what it relies on —
14	than pulling in a parser. If a plan's front-matter ever outgrows this reader,
15	that is itself signal the seam needs re-shaping.
16	
17	Usage:
18	    bin/walk-delivery-plan <plan-dir> [--json]
19	
20	Exit codes:
21	    0   walked cleanly and the derived counts equal the README oracle
22	    1   counts diverge from the README hand-count oracle (drift)
23	    2   a node/deliverable/oracle could not be parsed (seam not mechanical)
24	    64  usage error
25	"""
26	
27	from __future__ import annotations
28	
29	import json
30	import re
31	import sys
32	from pathlib import Path
33	
34	# --- schema constants (read off docs/delivery-shape-contract.md) -------------
35	
36	DELIVERABLE_DIR_RE = re.compile(r"^D(\d+)-")
37	NODE_FILE_RE = re.compile(r"^N(\d+)-.*\.md$")
38	DELIVERABLE_FILE = "_deliverable.md"
39	TASK_RE = re.compile(r"^\s*- \[([ xX])\]\s*(.*)$")
40	SKELETON_TAG = "`skeleton`"
41	
42	# Required front-matter keys per layer. A file missing any of these is
43	# "unparseable" — the seam is not mechanical, so we stop the line (exit 2).
44	REQUIRED = {
45	    "deliverable": ("layer", "id", "serves_kr", "maps_to"),
46	    "node": ("layer", "id", "type", "serves_kr", "maps_to", "completion.form"),
47	}
48	
49	
50	class PlanError(Exception):
51	    """A structural / parse failure: the file-set does not walk mechanically."""
52	
53	
54	# --- front-matter reader -----------------------------------------------------
55	
56	def _dequote(value: str) -> str:
57	    if len(value) >= 2 and value[0] == value[-1] and value[0] in "\"'":
58	        return value[1:-1]
59	    return value
60	
61	
62	def read_front_matter(path: Path) -> dict[str, str]:
63	    """Extract the contract's front-matter keys from a layer file.
64	
65	    Top-level scalars are read at column 0; the one nested key the manifest
66	    needs (`completion.form`) is read from the indented `form:` line under
67	    `completion:`. Block-scalar prose (`>` / `|`) is indented, so it never
68	    matches the column-0 key pattern and cannot pollute the result.
69	    """
70	    try:
71	        lines = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines()
72	    except OSError as exc:
73	        raise PlanError(f"{path}: cannot read ({exc})") from exc
74	
75	    if not lines or lines[0].strip() != "---":
76	        raise PlanError(f"{path}: missing opening '---' front-matter fence")
77	
78	    end = next((i for i in range(1, len(lines)) if lines[i].strip() == "---"), None)
79	    if end is None:
80	        raise PlanError(f"{path}: front-matter fence not closed with '---'")
81	    block = lines[1:end]
82	
83	    fm: dict[str, str] = {}
84	    for line in block:
85	        m = re.match(r"^([A-Za-z_][\w]*):(?:[ \t]+(.*))?$", line)
86	        if m and m.group(2):
87	            value = m.group(2).strip()
88	            if value not in (">", "|"):
89	                fm[m.group(1)] = _dequote(value)
90	
91	    in_completion = False
92	    for line in block:
93	        if re.match(r"^completion:\s*$", line):
94	            in_completion = True
95	            continue
96	        if in_completion:
97	            if re.match(r"^\S", line):  # dedent to column 0 — left the block
98	                break
99	            m = re.match(r"^\s+form:[ \t]+(.+)$", line)
100	            if m:
101	                fm["completion.form"] = _dequote(m.group(1).strip())
102	                break
103	    return fm
104	
105	
106	def require_keys(fm: dict[str, str], layer: str, path: Path) -> None:
107	    missing = [k for k in REQUIRED[layer] if k not in fm]
108	    if missing:
109	        raise PlanError(f"{path}: {layer} missing required front-matter {missing}")
110	
111	
112	# --- task (sub-issue) reader -------------------------------------------------
113	
114	def read_tasks(path: Path) -> list[dict]:
115	    tasks: list[dict] = []
116	    for line in path.read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines():
117	        m = TASK_RE.match(line)
118	        if not m:
119	            continue
120	        text = m.group(2).strip()
121	        skeleton = text.startswith(SKELETON_TAG)
122	        if skeleton:
123	            # The inline marker is promoted to the structured `skeleton` field;
124	            # strip it (and its `— ` / `- ` separator) so the text is the bare task.
125	            text = text[len(SKELETON_TAG):].lstrip(" —-").strip()
126	        tasks.append({
127	            "done": m.group(1).lower() == "x",
128	            "skeleton": skeleton,
129	            "text": text,
130	        })
131	    return tasks
132	
133	
134	# --- walk --------------------------------------------------------------------
135	
136	def _order(pattern: re.Pattern, name: str) -> int:
137	    m = pattern.match(name)
138	    return int(m.group(1)) if m else 0
139	
140	
141	def walk(root: Path) -> list[dict]:
142	    """Walk the plan into milestones → issues → sub-issues."""
143	    deliverable_dirs = sorted(
144	        (d for d in root.iterdir() if d.is_dir() and DELIVERABLE_DIR_RE.match(d.name)),
145	        key=lambda d: _order(DELIVERABLE_DIR_RE, d.name),
146	    )
147	    if not deliverable_dirs:
148	        raise PlanError(f"{root}: no deliverable directories (D<n>-*) found")
149	
150	    milestones: list[dict] = []
151	    for ddir in deliverable_dirs:
152	        dfile = ddir / DELIVERABLE_FILE
153	        if not dfile.exists():
154	            raise PlanError(f"{ddir}: missing {DELIVERABLE_FILE}")
155	        dfm = read_front_matter(dfile)
156	        if dfm.get("layer") != "deliverable":
157	            raise PlanError(f"{dfile}: layer is not 'deliverable'")
158	        require_keys(dfm, "deliverable", dfile)
159	
160	        node_files = sorted(
161	            (f for f in ddir.iterdir() if NODE_FILE_RE.match(f.name)),
162	            key=lambda f: _order(NODE_FILE_RE, f.name),
163	        )
164	        issues: list[dict] = []
165	        for nfile in node_files:
166	            nfm = read_front_matter(nfile)
167	            if nfm.get("layer") != "node":
168	                raise PlanError(f"{nfile}: layer is not 'node'")
169	            require_keys(nfm, "node", nfile)
170	            tasks = read_tasks(nfile)
171	            issues.append({
172	                "id": nfm["id"],
173	                "title": nfm.get("title", ""),
174	                "type": nfm["type"],
175	                "serves_kr": nfm["serves_kr"],
176	                "completion_form": nfm["completion.form"],
177	                "maps_to": nfm["maps_to"],
178	                "skeleton": nfm.get("skeleton", "").lower() == "true",
179	                "tracker_ref": nfm.get("tracker_ref"),
180	                "path": str(nfile.relative_to(root)),
181	                "sub_issues": tasks,
182	            })
183	        milestones.append({
184	            "id": dfm["id"],
185	            "title": dfm.get("title", ""),
186	            "serves_kr": dfm["serves_kr"],
187	            "maps_to": dfm["maps_to"],
188	            "path": str(dfile.relative_to(root)),
189	            "issues": issues,
190	        })
191	    return milestones
192	
193	
194	def counts(milestones: list[dict]) -> dict[str, int]:
195	    issues = [i for m in milestones for i in m["issues"]]
196	    return {
197	        "milestones": len(milestones),
198	        "issues": len(issues),
199	        "sub_issues": sum(len(i["sub_issues"]) for i in issues),
200	    }
201	
202	
203	# --- README oracle -----------------------------------------------------------
204	
205	ORACLE_ROW_RE = re.compile(r"^\|([^|]+)\|[^|]*\|\s*\*\*(\d+)\*\*\s*\|")
206	
207	
208	def read_oracle(root: Path) -> dict[str, int]:
209	    """Read the hand-count manifest table from the plan's root README."""
210	    readme = root / "README.md"
211	    if not readme.exists():
212	        raise PlanError(f"{root}: no README.md (the hand-count oracle is missing)")
213	    expected: dict[str, int] = {}
214	    for line in readme.read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines():
215	        m = ORACLE_ROW_RE.match(line)
216	        if not m:
217	            continue
218	        label = m.group(1).strip().lower()
219	        n = int(m.group(2))
220	        if "sub-issue" in label:
221	            expected["sub_issues"] = n
222	        elif "milestone" in label:
223	            expected["milestones"] = n
224	        elif "issue" in label:
225	            expected["issues"] = n
226	    missing = [k for k in ("milestones", "issues", "sub_issues") if k not in expected]
227	    if missing:
228	        raise PlanError(f"{readme}: hand-count manifest table missing rows {missing}")
229	    return expected
230	
231	
232	# --- rendering ---------------------------------------------------------------
233	
234	def render_text(root: Path, milestones: list[dict], derived: dict, oracle: dict, ok: bool) -> str:
235	    out: list[str] = []
236	    out.append(f"Delivery-plan manifest — {root.name}")
237	    out.append(f"source: {root}")
238	    out.append("")
239	    for m in milestones:
240	        out.append(f"MILESTONE  {m['id']}  {m['title']}")
241	        out.append(f"           [{m['maps_to']} · serves {m['serves_kr']}]")
242	        for i in m["issues"]:
243	            flags = [i["type"], i["serves_kr"]]
244	            if i["skeleton"]:
245	                flags.append("skeleton")
246	            if i["tracker_ref"]:
247	                flags.append(i["tracker_ref"])
248	            out.append(f"  ISSUE  {i['id']}  {i['title']}")
249	            out.append(f"         [{i['maps_to']} · {' · '.join(flags)}]")
250	            out.append(f"         completion: {i['completion_form']}")
251	            for t in i["sub_issues"]:
252	                box = "x" if t["done"] else " "
253	                tag = " (skeleton)" if t["skeleton"] else ""
254	                text = t["text"]
255	                if len(text) > 76:
256	                    text = text[:73] + "..."
257	                out.append(f"    SUB-ISSUE  [{box}]{tag} {text}")
258	        out.append("")
259	    out.append(
260	        f"Summary: {derived['milestones']} milestones / "
261	        f"{derived['issues']} issues / {derived['sub_issues']} sub-issues"
262	    )
263	    out.append(
264	        f"Oracle (README hand-count): {oracle['milestones']} / "
265	        f"{oracle['issues']} / {oracle['sub_issues']}  -> {'MATCH' if ok else 'MISMATCH'}"
266	    )
267	    return "\n".join(out)
268	
269	
270	def build_json(root: Path, milestones: list[dict], derived: dict, oracle: dict, ok: bool) -> str:
271	    return json.dumps({
272	        "source": str(root),
273	        "milestones": milestones,
274	        "summary": derived,
275	        "oracle": oracle,
276	        "oracle_match": ok,
277	    }, indent=2)
278	
279	
280	# --- entrypoint --------------------------------------------------------------
281	
282	def main(argv: list[str]) -> int:
283	    args = [a for a in argv[1:] if not a.startswith("--")]
284	    as_json = "--json" in argv[1:]
285	    if len(args) != 1:
286	        sys.stderr.write("usage: walk-delivery-plan <plan-dir> [--json]\n")
287	        return 64
288	    root = Path(args[0])
289	    if not root.is_dir():
290	        sys.stderr.write(f"error: {root} is not a directory\n")
291	        return 64
292	
293	    try:
294	        milestones = walk(root)
295	        oracle = read_oracle(root)
296	    except PlanError as exc:
297	        sys.stderr.write(f"PARSE ERROR: {exc}\n")
298	        return 2
299	
300	    derived = counts(milestones)
301	    ok = derived == oracle
302	
303	    if as_json:
304	        print(build_json(root, milestones, derived, oracle, ok))
305	    else:
306	        print(render_text(root, milestones, derived, oracle, ok))
307	
308	    if not ok:
309	        sys.stderr.write(
310	            f"\nDRIFT: derived {derived} != README oracle {oracle}. "
311	            "The file-set drifted from its hand-count, or the layout does not "
312	            "walk deterministically. Investigate before declaring the gate passed.\n"
313	        )
314	        return 1
315	    return 0
316	
317	
318	if __name__ == "__main__":
319	    raise SystemExit(main(sys.argv))
320	
```

(JSON output carries it automatically via the issue dict.)\n\n## Linear changes\n\n### E. ABA-285 (N04) — tighten acceptance criteria\nAdd a third \"Done when\": *the emitted plan sets `delegates_to` on every node (walk-script enforces presence; exit 2 if missing), and the skill's emitted-issue guidance documents deferred, build-time delegation per the contract's \"Delegation — timing & surfacing\" section.* (Note: enforcement now lives in `walk-delivery-plan`, so this is verifiable immediately — it does not wait on `check-plan-framing`.)\n\n### F. ABA-287 (N06) — repoint the pinned comment\nUpdate the existing decision comment to cite `docs/delivery-shape-contract.md` § *Delegation — timing & surfacing* as the durable home, so the rule no longer lives only in a comment.\n\n### G. `agent-skills-workflow` adapter — backlog issue (team ABA, no project, label `repo:agent-skills-workflow`)\nTitle: *Adapter: surface node `delegates_to` as an on-pickup instruction on the emitted issue.*\nBody: render each node's `delegates_to` (carried in the walk-delivery-plan manifest) into the emitted Linear issue as an `▶ On pickup` instruction; add an adapter check that fails if any non-ktlo issue lacks it; ktlo → \"no breakdown step.\" Reference the contract section and the six worked-example issues (ABA-285…290) as the target output.\n\n## Execution order\n\n1.
